\displaystyle\pi^{Y}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert z-y\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}z}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z)\,\mathrm{d}z\cdot(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}}=\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert z-(y-h\alpha)\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}z}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z)\,\mathrm{d}z\cdot(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}}\,\exp\bigl(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}y+\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{\ell(y)}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z)\,\mathrm{d}z}\exp\bigl(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}y+\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}\bigr)\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

We now identify an
effective domain
of
\pi^{Y}
, which takes
up most of measure of
\pi^{Y}
. Below,
\mathcal{K}_{\delta}:=\{x:d(x,\mathcal{K})\leq\delta\}

denotes the
\delta
-
blow up
of
\mathcal{K}
.

Lemma 2.12
.

In the setting of Problem
2.1
,
for
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}:=\mathcal{K}_{\delta}+h\alpha
, if
\delta\geq 13hn
,
then

\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq\exp\bigl(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2h}+13\delta n\bigr)\,.

Proof.

Using the density formula of
\pi^{Y}
in Lemma
2.11
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z}\,\mathrm{d}z\cdot\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}}\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c}}\int_{\mathcal{K}}e^{-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert z-(y-h\alpha)\rVert^{2}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}y+\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{=}\frac{1}{(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}}\int_{\mathcal{K}_{\delta}^{c}}\int_{\mathcal{K}}e^{-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert z-v\rVert^{2}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v-\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{1}{(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}}\int_{\mathcal{K}_{\delta}^{c}}\int_{\mathcal{H}(v)}e^{-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert z-v\rVert^{2}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v-\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{K}_{\delta}^{c}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v-\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\Bigl(\int_{d(v,\mathcal{K})}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2h}}}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v=:(\#)\,,

where
(i)
follows from the change of variables by
v=y-h\alpha
,
and in
(ii)
for
v\in\mathcal{K}_{\delta}^{c}
,
\mathcal{H}(v)
denotes the
supporting half-space at
\mathsf{proj}_{\mathcal{K}}(v)
containing
\mathcal{K}
,
where
\mathsf{proj}_{\mathcal{K}}(v)
denotes the projection of
v
to
\mathcal{K}
.
Denoting the CDF of the standard Gaussian by
\Phi
, we have

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\int_{s}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{t^{2}}{2h}}\,\mathrm{d}t=1-\Phi(h^{-1/2}s)=:\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}s)\,.

By the co-area formula and integration by parts, for the
n
-dimensional
Hausdorff measure
\mathcal{H}^{n}
,

\displaystyle(\#)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{coarea}}{=}e^{-\frac{1}{2}h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}s)\int_{\partial\mathcal{K}_{s}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\underset{\text{IBP}}{=}e^{-\frac{1}{2}h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\Bigl(\Bigl[\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}s)\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\partial\mathcal{K}_{t}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t\Bigr]_{s=\delta}^{\infty}+\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2h}}}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{\partial\mathcal{K}_{t}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}v)\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}s\Bigr)

\displaystyle=e^{-\frac{1}{2}h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2}}\Bigl(\underbrace{\Bigl[\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}s)\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}\backslash\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v}\,\mathrm{d}v\Bigr]_{s=\delta}^{\infty}}_{=:\mathsf{I}}+\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2h}}}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}\backslash\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v}\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}s\Bigr)\,.

(2.5)

As shown later in Lemma
2.13
, this quantity
is bounded by
e^{13sn}\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v
. From
a standard bound on
\Phi^{c}

[
RW00
, Theorem 20]

given by

\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}s)\leq\frac{\exp(-\frac{s^{2}}{2h})}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\quad\text{for any }s>0\,,

(2.6)

it follows that
\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}s)\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}\backslash\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v}\,\mathrm{d}v

vanishes at
s=\infty
, and thus the term
\mathsf{I}
can bounded by

0
.

Putting the bound in Lemma
2.13
into (
2.5
),

\int_{\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z}\,\mathrm{d}z\cdot\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\,e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2h}}e^{13sn}\,\mathrm{d}s\cdot\int_{\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z}\,\mathrm{d}z\,.

Dividing both sides by
\int_{\mathcal{K}}e^{-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}z}\,\mathrm{d}z
and
invoking the standard bound on
\Phi^{c}
again, we obtain

\displaystyle\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\,e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2h}}e^{13sn}\mathrm{d}s=e^{52hn^{2}}\Bigl(1-\Phi\bigl(\frac{\delta-13hn}{h^{1/2}}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq e^{52hn^{2}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{(\delta-13hn)^{2}}{2h}\bigr)=\exp\bigl(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2h}+13\delta n\bigr)\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

This analytical property suggests taking
\delta\asymp 1/n
and
h\asymp 1/n^{2}
.
Thus, we set
h=\tfrac{c}{13^{2}n^{2}}
and
\delta=\tfrac{t}{13n}

for parameters
c,t>0
, under which
\delta\geq 13hn
turns into

t\geq c
, and

\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+t\bigr)\,.

We now prove an interesting result quantifying how fast
\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v

increases in
s
.

Lemma 2.13
.

In the setting of Problem
2.1
,

\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}\leq\exp(13sn)\,.

Proof.

Due to the assumption on the ground set
\mathsf{L}_{V,g}=\{V(x)-V_{0}\leq 10n\}
,
it holds that
\mathcal{K}\cap[t=V_{0}/n+10]
contains
B_{1}^{n}(x_{0})
.
Then, the definition of
\mathcal{K}
ensures that
\mathcal{K}
contains
B_{1}^{n+1}(x_{0},V_{0}/n+11)
.

We now bound the quantity of interest: for
t_{0}=11+V_{0}/n
,

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}=\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{s}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{\int_{(1+s)(\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1})}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{=}(1+s)^{n+1}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\exp(-nst)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}

\displaystyle\underset{(iii)}{\leq}e^{s(n+1)}e^{11sn}\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x}\leq e^{13sn}\,,

where
(i)
follows from
\mathcal{K}_{s}-t_{0}e_{n+1}=(\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1})_{s}\subset(1+s)(\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1})

due to
B_{1}(x_{0},0)\subset\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}
, and in
(ii)
we
used the change of variables by scaling the whole system by
(1+s)
,
and
(iii)
follows from
t\geq-11
in
\mathcal{K}-t_{0}e_{n+1}
.
∎

Per-step guarantees.

Using the properties established above, we provide concrete choices
of parameters, bounding a per-step query complexity for the backward
step under a
warm start
.

Lemma 2.14
.

In the setting of Problem
2.1
,
let
\mu
be an
M
-warm initial distribution for
\pi^{Z}
. Given

k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\eta\in(0,1)
, set
S=\frac{16kM}{\eta}(\geq 16)
,

h=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{(\log\log S)^{2}}{13^{2}\log S}
and
N=S\log^{2}S
.
Then, per iteration, the failure probability is at most
\eta/k
,
and the expected number of queries is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(M\log^{2}\tfrac{k}{\eta})
.

Proof.

We note that
\mu*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})
is
M
-warm with respect to

\pi^{Y}
due to
\mathrm{d}\mu/\mathrm{d}\pi^{X}\leq M
. Hence, the failure probability
per iteration is
\mathbb{E}_{\mu*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})}[(1-\ell)^{N}]\leq M\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{Y}}[(1-\ell)^{N}]
.
For
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}=\mathcal{K}_{\delta}+h\alpha
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{Y}}[(1-\ell)^{N}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c}}\cdot+\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap[\ell\geq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\cdot+\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap[\ell\leq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\cdot

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})+\int_{[\ell\geq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\exp(-\ell N)\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{Y}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap[\ell\leq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\frac{\ell(y)\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}y+\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2})}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+t\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\,\frac{\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}y+\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}y}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}

\displaystyle\underset{(iii)}{=}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+t\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\,\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{\delta}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v-\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}v}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}

\displaystyle\underset{(iv)}{\leq}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+t\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\,e^{13\delta n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{kM}\,,

where in
(i)
we used the density formula of
\pi^{Y}
(Lemma
2.11
),

(ii)
follows from Lemma
2.12
,
(iii)
is
due to the change of variables via
y=v+h\alpha
, in
(iv)
we used
Lemma
2.13
, and the last line follows from the
choices of
c=\frac{(\log\log S)^{2}}{13^{2}\log S}
,
t=\log\log S
,
and
N=S\log^{2}S
. Therefore,
\mathbb{E}_{\mu*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})}[(1-\ell)^{N}]\leq\eta/k
.
Note that under these choices of parameters, we have
t\geq c
and
so
\delta\geq 13hn
, as requested in Lemma
2.12
.

We now bound the expected number of trials until the first acceptance.
In a similar vein to above, the expected number is bounded by
M\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{Y}}[\ell^{-1}\wedge N]
,
where

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}\frac{1}{\ell}\wedge N\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{Y}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{1}{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}\pi^{Y}+N\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq\frac{\int_{\mathcal{K}_{\delta}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v-\frac{1}{2}\,h\lVert\alpha\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}v}{\int_{\mathcal{K}}\exp(-\alpha^{\mathsf{T}}v)\,\mathrm{d}v}+N\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+t\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq e^{t}+N\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+t\bigr)\leq\log S+\log^{2}S

\displaystyle\leq 2\log^{2}\frac{16kM}{\eta}\,.

Therefore, from an
M
-warm start, the expected number of queries
per iteration is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(M\log^{2}\tfrac{k}{\eta})
.
∎

Combining the results on the mixing and per-iteration complexity together,
we can conclude the query complexity of sampling from a logconcave
distribution, as claimed in Theorem
1.5
.

Theorem 2.15
.

In the setting of Problem
2.1
,
consider
\pi(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}}
given in (
exp-red
).
For any given
\eta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
q\geq 2
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
defined
below, the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with
h=(13^{2}n^{2}\log\frac{16kM}{\eta})^{-1}
,

N=\frac{16kM}{\eta}\log^{2}\frac{16kM}{\eta}
, and initial distribution

\mu_{0}
which is
M
-warm with respect to
\pi
achieves
\mathcal{R}_{q}(\mu_{k}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leq\varepsilon

(so
\mathcal{R}_{q}(\mu_{k}^{X}\mathbin{\|}\pi^{X})\leq\varepsilon
) after
k=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(qn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log^{2}\frac{M}{\eta\varepsilon})

iterations, where
\mu_{k}
is the law of
k
-th iterate, and
\Sigma_{x}

is the covariance matrix of
\pi^{X}\propto\exp(-V)
. With probability

1-\eta
, the algorithm iterates
k
times successfully, using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(qMn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log^{4}\frac{1}{\eta\varepsilon})

evaluation queries in expectation. Moreover, an
M
-warm start for

\pi^{X}
can be used to generate an
M
-warm start for
\pi
.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.5
, the

\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
should iterate

k=\mathcal{O}\bigl(qh^{-1}C_{\mathsf{PI}}(\pi)\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon}\bigr)=\mathcal{O}\bigl(qh^{-1}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon}\bigr)\underset{\text{Remark \ref{rem:self-dependence}}}{=}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigl(qn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log^{2}\frac{M}{\eta\varepsilon}\bigr)

times to achieve
\varepsilon
-distance in
\mathcal{R}_{q}
. Under the choice
of
h
and
N
as claimed, each iteration succeeds with probability

1-\nicefrac{{k}}{{\eta}}
and uses
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(M\log^{2}\nicefrac{{k}}{{\eta}})

queries in expectation by Lemma
2.14
. Therefore,
throughout
k
iterations, the algorithm succeeds with probability

1-\eta
, using

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigl(qMn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log^{4}\frac{1}{\eta\varepsilon}\bigr)

evaluation queries in expectation.

Next, an
M
-warm start
\mu^{X}
for
\pi^{X}
can be lifted to
an
M
-warm distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
for
\pi
through the following
procedure: generate
x\sim\mu^{X}
and draw
t\sim\pi^{T|X=x}\propto e^{-nt}\cdot\mathds{1}[t\geq\nicefrac{{V(x)}}{{n}}]
,
obtaining
(x,t)
. Note that
\pi^{T|X=x}
can be sampled easily
by drawing
u\sim\textnormal{Unif}\,([0,1])
and taking
F^{-1}(u)
, where
F

is the CDF of
\pi^{T|X=x}
. Then, the density of the law of
(x,t)

is
\mu^{X}\cdot\pi^{T|X}
, and it follows from
\pi(x,t)=\pi^{X}\cdot\pi^{T|X}

that

\frac{\mu^{X}\pi^{T|X}}{\pi}=\frac{\mu^{X}}{\pi^{X}}\leq M\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.16

(Self-dependence of parameteres)
.

Upon
substitution of the chosen
h
, the required number of iterations
would be

k\gtrsim qn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{kM}{\eta}\,,

where
k
shows up in both sides. When
c\geq 1
, since
x\geq c\log x

holds for
x\geq 2c\log c
, the above bound on
k
can be turned
into

k\gtrsim qn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon}\log\bigl(qn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\frac{M}{\eta}\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon}\bigr)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigl(qn^{2}(\lVert\Sigma_{x}\rVert\vee 1)\log\frac{M}{\varepsilon}\log\frac{M}{\eta}\bigr)\,.

3
Warm-start generation via tilted Gaussian cooling

In the previous section, we assumed access to an
M
-warm distribution
for
\pi(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}}
. Here we address the problem
of generating a warm start (i.e.,
M=\mathcal{O}(1)
).

Problem 3.1

(Warm-start generation)
.

Given
access to a well-defined function oracle
\mathsf{Eval}_{x_{0},R}(V)
for
convex
V:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\overline{\mathbb{R}}
, what is the complexity of generating an

\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm start
\mu^{X}
with respect to
\pi^{X}
(i.e.,
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\mu^{X}\mathbin{\|}\pi^{X})=\mathcal{O}(1)
)?

Compared to Problem
2.1
, this
requires

access to
x_{0}
and
R
. We prove
Result 2
in this section:

Theorem 3.2

(Restatement of Theorem
1.6
)
.

In the setting of
Problem
3.1
, there exists an algorithm
that for given
\eta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\eta

returns a sample
X_{*}
satisfying
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\operatorname{law}X_{*}\mathbin{\|}\pi^{X})\leq\varepsilon
,
using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(R^{2}\vee n)\operatorname{polylog}\nicefrac{{1}}{{\eta\varepsilon}})

evaluation queries in expectation. In particular, if
\pi^{X}
is
well-rounded (i.e.,
R^{2}\asymp n
), then we need
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3}\operatorname{polylog}\nicefrac{{1}}{{\eta\varepsilon}})

queries in expectation.

As in the previous section, we tackle this problem after reducing
it to the exponential distribution through (
exp-red
),
and then truncate this distribution to a convex domain.

Convex truncation.

Using Lemma
A.7
with
\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{X}}[\lVert X-x_{0}\rVert^{2}]\leq R^{2}
,
we have that
\text{for any }l\geq 1
,

\mathbb{P}_{\pi^{X}}(\lVert X-x_{0}\rVert\geq Rl)\leq\exp(-l+1).

In addition, since
\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{T}}[\lvert T-\mu_{t}\rvert^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\pi}[\lvert T-\mu_{t}\rvert^{2}]\leq 160

(Lemma
2.5
), we also have

\mathbb{P}_{\pi^{T}}(\lvert T-\mu_{t}\rvert\geq 13l)\leq\exp(-l+1)\quad\text{for any }l\geq 1\,.

Putting these two together and using
\mu_{t}\leq V_{0}/n+5\leq V(x_{0})/n+5

(Lemma
2.4
), for
\varepsilon>0
,
l=\log\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}
,
and
B=\frac{V(x_{0})}{n}+5+13l
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\pi}\bigl\{\bigl(B_{Rl}^{n}(x_{0})\times(-\infty,B]\bigr)^{c}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{\pi}\bigl\{\bigl(B_{Rl}^{n}(x_{0})\times\mathbb{R}\bigr)^{c}\bigr\}+\mathbb{P}_{\pi}\bigl\{\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\times[B,\infty)\bigr)^{c}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\pi^{X}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{n}\backslash B_{Rl}^{n}(x_{0})\bigr)+\mathbb{P}_{\pi^{T}}\bigl(\mathbb{R}\backslash[B,\infty)\bigr)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}=\varepsilon\,.

Thus,
\bar{\mathcal{K}}:=\mathcal{K}\cap\{B_{Rl}(x_{0})\times(-\infty,B]\}
takes
up
(1-\varepsilon)
measure of
\pi
, and
B_{Rl}(x_{0})\times(V_{0}/n,B]

could be thought of as the
essential domain
of
\pi
. Moreover,
since
x_{0}\in\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},V}
(i.e.,
V(x_{0})-V_{0}\leq 10n
),

\frac{V(x_{0})}{n}+5+13l-\frac{V_{0}}{n}\leq 15+13l\,,

so the diameter of
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
is
\mathcal{O}(lR)
. Going forward, we use

\bar{\pi}
to denote
\pi|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
.

3.1
Tilted Gaussian Cooling

We would like to find a sequence
\{\mu_{i}\}_{0\leq i\leq m}
of
distributions, where
\mu_{0}
is easy to sample,
\mu_{m}
is our
target, and
\mu_{i}
is
\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm with respect to
\mu_{i+1}
.
Then, as
\mu_{i}
is warm for
\mu_{i+1}
, we can run an efficient
sampler to go from
\mu_{i}
to
\mu_{i+1}
.

\mathsf{Gaussian\ Cooling}
, introduced in
[
CV18
]
, can generate an

\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm start for the uniform distribution over a convex body.
However, extending this to general logconcave distributions has remained
elusive. A straightforward extension to the exponential distribution

\bar{\pi}
fails due to an exponential change in the value of
e^{-nt}

in
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
; roughly speaking, while
t
may vary by at most
\Theta(l)
,
the density can become exponentially small relative to its maximum.
This significant change in value prevents us from achieving
\mathcal{O}(1)
-warmness,
necessitating additional ideas to address this challenge.

We propose
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
(Algorithm
2
), which handles the

x
-direction through the
\mathsf{Gaussian\ Cooling}
algorithm and the
t
-direction
through an exponential tilt. Its annealing distributions have two
parameters
\sigma^{2}
and
\rho
, given as

\mu_{\sigma^{2},\rho}(x,t)\propto\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x-x_{0}\rVert^{2}-\rho t\bigr)\big|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,.

We can translate the whole system by
(x,t)\mapsto(x-x_{0},t-\frac{V(x_{0})}{n}-11)

so that the shifted
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
contains a unit ball centered at the
origin (i.e.,
x_{0}=0
), and
t
is within
[-21,13l-6]
over

\bar{\mathcal{K}}
.

We develop in §
3.2
a sampling algorithm
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}

for these annealing distributions. Assuming this sampler is given,
we provide an outline of
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
. For the annealing distribution
\mu_{i}:=\mu_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\rho_{i}}
,
we use
\bar{\mu}_{i}
to denote an approximate distribution close
to
\mu_{i}
produced by the sampler.
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
has two main annealing
phases:

•

Initialization (
\sigma^{2}=n^{-1}
and
\rho=l^{-1}
)

–

Initial distribution:
\mu_{0},\bar{\mu}_{0}\propto\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert)^{2}|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}

(run rejection sampling with proposal
\mathcal{N}(0,n^{-1}I_{n})\otimes\text{Unif}\,([-21,13l-6])
)

–

Target distribution:
\mu_{1}\propto\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-t/l)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
.

•

Phase I (
n^{-1}\leq\sigma^{2}\leq 1
and
l^{-1}\leq\rho\leq n
)

–

Run the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with initial
\bar{\mu}_{i}
, target
\mu_{i+1}
,
and accuracy
\log 2
in
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
, where

\sigma_{i+1}^{2}=\sigma_{i}^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{1}{n}\bigr)\quad\&\quad\rho_{i+1}=\rho_{i}\bigl(1+\frac{1}{ln}\bigr)\,.

•

Phase II (
1\leq\sigma^{2}\leq l^{2}R^{2}
)

–

Run the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with initial
\bar{\mu}_{i}
, target
\mu_{i+1}
,
and accuracy
\log 2
in
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
, where

\sigma_{i+1}^{2}=\sigma_{i}^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{l^{2}R^{2}}\bigr)\,.

•

Termination
(\sigma^{2}=l^{2}R^{2})

–

Run the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with initial
\mu_{l^{2}R^{2},n}(x,t)
and target

\bar{\pi}\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
.

Input:
convex
\mathcal{K}=\{(x,t):V(x)\leq nt\}
,
accuracy
\varepsilon>0
, target dist.
\pi(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}}

s.t.
B_{1}^{n}(x_{0})\subset\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\pi}[\lVert X-x_{0}\rVert^{2}]\leq R^{2}
.

Output:
a sample
z
with
law approximately close to
\pi
.

1:

Let
\bar{\mathcal{K}}=\mathcal{K}\cap\bigl(B_{Rl}^{n}(x_{0})\times(-\infty,V(x_{0})/n+5+13l]\bigr)

with
l=\log\frac{6}{\varepsilon}
.

2:

Draw
z_{0}\sim\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x-x_{0}\rVert^{2})|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}

via rejection sampling with proposal
\mathcal{N}(x_{0},n^{-1}I_{n})\otimes\text{Unif}\,\bigl((V(x_{0})/n-10,V(x_{0})/n+5+13l]\bigr)
.

3:

Denote
\mu_{\sigma^{2},\rho}:=\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x-x_{0}\rVert^{2}-\rho t)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}

for
\sigma^{2}=n^{-1}
and
\rho=l^{-1}
.

4:

Get
z\sim\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with initial
z_{0}
, target
\mu_{\sigma^{2},\rho}
,
accuracy
\log 2
(in
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
), and success prob.
\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(l^{2}R^{2}\vee n)}
.

5:

while

\sigma^{2}\in[n^{-1},l^{2}R^{2}]
and
\rho\in[l^{-1},n]

do

6:

Sample
z
from
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with the same setup. Then update

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\leftarrow\sigma^{2}(1+\frac{1}{n})\quad\&\quad\rho\leftarrow\min\bigl(n,\rho\,(1+\frac{1}{ln})\bigr)\ \text{when }\sigma^{2}\in\bigl[\frac{1}{n},1\bigr],\,\rho\in\bigl[\frac{1}{l},n\bigr]

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\leftarrow\sigma^{2}(1+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{l^{2}R^{2}})\ \text{when }\sigma^{2}\in[1,l^{2}R^{2}]\,.

7:

end

while

8:

Return
z\sim\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with initial
\mu_{l^{2}R^{2},n}
,
target
\pi|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
, accuracy
\varepsilon/3
, and success
prob.
\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(l^{2}R^{2}\vee n)}
.

Algorithm 2

\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}

3.2
Proximal sampler for annealing distributions

We employ
\mathsf{PS}
to sample from the annealing distributions
\{\mu_{\sigma^{2},\rho}\}
.
For
v:=(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
and
w:=(y,s)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
,
\mathsf{PS}

for this target and variance
h
, called
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
(Algorithm
3
),
can be implemented by repeating

•

[Forward]
w\sim\mu^{W|V=v}=\mathcal{N}(v,hI_{n+1})
.

•

[Backward] For
\tau:=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{h+\sigma^{2}}<1
,
y_{\tau}:=\tau y
,
and
h_{\tau}:=\tau h
, run

v\sim\mu^{V|W=w}\propto\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v\rVert^{2}\bigr)\big|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\propto\bigl[\mathcal{N}(y_{\tau},h_{\tau}I_{n})\times\mathcal{N}(s-\rho h,h)\bigr]\big|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,,

where the backward step is implemented by rejection sampling with
the proposal
\mathcal{N}(y_{\tau},h_{\tau}I_{n})\times\mathcal{N}(s-\rho h,h)
.
Note that
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
may be thought of as a special case of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}

with
\sigma^{2}=\infty
. As in §
2.2
, we analyze
its mixing and per-step complexity.

Input:
initial pt.
v_{0}\sim\pi_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n+1})
,
truncated
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, threshold
N
, variances

h
, parameters
\sigma^{2},\rho>0
.

Output:

v_{k+1}=(x_{k+1},t_{k+1})
.

1:

Let
\tau=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{h+\sigma^{2}}
. Denote
v=(x,t)

and
w=(y,s)
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}
.

2:

for

i=0,\dotsc,k

do

3:

Sample
w_{i+1}\sim\mathcal{N}(v_{i},hI_{n+1})
.

4:

Sample
v_{i+1}\sim\mathsf{G}(w_{i+1})|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
for
\mathsf{G}(w):=\mathcal{N}(\tau y,\tau hI_{n})\times\mathcal{N}(s-\rho h,h)
.

5:

\quad(\uparrow)
Repeat
v_{i+1}\sim\mathsf{G}(w_{i+1})
until

v_{i+1}\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}
. If
\#
attempts
i
\,\geq N
, declare

Failure
.

6:

end

for

Algorithm 3

\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}

3.2.1
Convergence rate

For
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
, we use a mixing result based on (

LSI

), rather
than (

PI

). As opposed to the exponential distribution, the
annealing distributions have
C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\mu)<\infty
, so we can now invoke
the (

LSI

)-mixing result in Lemma
2.9
.
Therefore, for
q\geq 1
, the kernel
P
of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
, and initial
distribution
\nu
with
\nu\ll\mu
, it holds that

\mathcal{R}_{q}(\nu P\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\frac{\mathcal{R}_{q}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\mu)}{\bigl(1+h/C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\mu)\bigr)^{2/q}}\,.

(3.1)

We provide a bound on
C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\mu)
, using the Bakry-Émery criterion
and bounded perturbation.

Lemma 3.3
.

C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\mu)\lesssim\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2}

for
\mu(x,t)\propto\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
.

Proof.

Consider a probability measure defined by

\nu(x,t)\propto\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2l^{2}}\,t^{2}-\rho t\bigr)\,.

It is
\min(\sigma^{-2},l^{-2})
-strongly logconcave, so
C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\nu)\leq\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2}

by the Bakry-Émery criterion (Lemma
A.1
). It
is well known that a convex truncation of strongly logconcave measures
only helps in satisfying the criterion, so
C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\nu|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}})\leq\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2}

[
Wan13
, Theorem 3.3.2]
. Since

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu}{\mathrm{d}\nu|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}}=\mathds{1}[(x,t)\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}]\exp\bigl(\frac{t^{2}}{2l^{2}}\bigr)\,\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2l^{2}}\,t^{2}-\rho t)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)}\,,

and
t\in[-21,13l-6]
over
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
, we have

\exp\bigl(-\Theta(1)\bigr)\leq\inf_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu}{\mathrm{d}\nu|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}}\leq\sup_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu}{\mathrm{d}\nu|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}}\leq\exp\bigl(\Theta(1)\bigr)\,.

By the Holley–Stroock perturbation principle (Lemma
A.2
),

C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\mu)\leq\exp\bigl(\Theta(1)\bigr)\,C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\nu|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}})\lesssim\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2}\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

Combining the exponential contraction (
3.1
)
and the bound on the LSI constant, we conclude that if
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
started
at an initial distribution
\nu
iterates
\mathcal{O}(qh^{-1}C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\mu)\log\frac{\mathcal{R}_{q}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\mu)}{\varepsilon})=\mathcal{O}(qh^{-1}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log\frac{\mathcal{R}_{q}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\mu)}{\varepsilon})

times, then it achieves
\varepsilon
-distance within the target
\nu

in
\mathcal{R}_{q}
-divergence.

We can strengthen this
\mathcal{R}_{q}
-divergence result to the stronger

\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
-divergence by following the boosting scheme used
in
[
KZ25
]
, which stems from a classical result in
[
DMLM03
]
.

Lemma 3.4

(
[
KZ25
, Theorem 14]
)
.

Consider
a Markov chain with initial distribution
\nu
and kernel
P
reversible
with respect to a stationary distribution
\mu
. Then,

\bigl\|\frac{\mathrm{d}(\nu P^{k})}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-1\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\,\bigl\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-1\bigr\|_{L^{\infty}}\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{x}\lVert\delta_{x}P^{k}-\mu\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}\,,

and in particular,
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu P^{k}\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq 2\,\lVert\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-1\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{x}\lVert\delta_{x}P^{k}-\mu\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}
.

In words, a uniform bound on the
\mathsf{TV}
-distance from
any start

implies the Rényi-infinity bound. To use this result, we establish
the uniform ergodicity of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
.

Lemma 3.5
.

For given
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let
P

be the Markov kernel of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with variance
h
and target distribution

\mu
. Then,
\sup_{v_{0}\in\mathcal{\bar{K}}}\lVert\delta_{v_{0}}P^{k}-\mu\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}\leq\varepsilon

for
k=\mathcal{O}(h^{-1}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log\frac{n+h^{-1}R^{2}}{\varepsilon})
.
If an initial distribution
\nu
is
M
-warm with respect to
\mu
,
then
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu P^{k}\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\varepsilon
after
k=\mathcal{O}(h^{-1}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log\frac{M(n+h^{-1}R^{2})}{\varepsilon})

iterations.

Proof.

We first bound the warmness of
\delta_{v_{0}}P
for
\mu
. By the
definition of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
, the forward step brings
\delta_{v_{0}}

to
\mathcal{N}(v_{0},hI_{n+1})
, and then the backward step
\pi^{V|W=(y,s)}(x,t)\propto\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}\,}\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t-\frac{1}{2h}\lVert w-v\rVert^{2})|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}

brings it to, for
v=(x,t)
and
w=(y,s)

(\delta_{v_{0}}P)(x,t)=\mathds{1}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}(x,t)\,\int\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v\rVert^{2})\exp(-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v_{0}\rVert^{2})}{(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert(y,s)-(z,l)\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}l}\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}s\,.

For
D:=\text{diam}\,\bar{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim Rl
, it follows from Young’s
inequality (
\lVert a-b\rVert^{2}\leq 3\,\lVert a\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\,\lVert b\rVert^{2}
)
that

\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert(y,s)-(z,l)\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{3}{4h}\,\lVert(y,s)\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{2h}\,\lVert(z,l)\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{3}{4h}\,\lVert(y,s)\rVert^{2}+\frac{3D^{2}}{2h}\,.

Hence,

\delta_{v_{0}}P\leq\frac{\exp(\frac{3D^{2}}{2h})\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)}{(2\pi h)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l)\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}l}\int\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v_{0}\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{4h}\,\lVert w\rVert^{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}s\,.

The exponent of the integrand can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v_{0}\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{4h}\,\lVert w\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4h}\,\lVert w-2(v+v_{0})\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert v\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert v_{0}\rVert^{2}+\frac{1}{h}\,\lVert v+v_{0}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4h}\,\lVert w-2(v+v_{0})\rVert^{2}+\frac{4D^{2}}{h}\,,

so it follows that

\delta_{v_{0}}P\leq\frac{2^{(n+1)/2}\exp(\frac{6D^{2}}{h})\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l)\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}l}\,.

Dividing this by
\mu(x,t)\propto\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
,
we have

\frac{\delta_{v_{0}}P}{\mu}\leq 2^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\exp(6h^{-1}D^{2})\,,

which implies
\mathcal{R}_{q}(\delta_{v_{0}}P\mathbin{\|}\mu)\lesssim n+h^{-1}D^{2}
.
Thus,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
can achieve
\mathcal{R}_{q}(\delta_{v_{0}}P^{k}\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\varepsilon

for
k=\mathcal{O}(qh^{-1}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log\frac{n+h^{-1}D^{2}}{\varepsilon})
,
and due to
2\,\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}^{2}\leq\lim_{q\downarrow 1}\mathcal{R}_{q}
,

\sup_{v_{0}\in\mathcal{\bar{K}}}\lVert\delta_{v_{0}}P^{k}-\mu\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}\leq\varepsilon\,.

For the second claim, replacing
\varepsilon
with
\varepsilon/M
and using
Lemma
3.4
with
\bigl\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-1\bigr\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq M
,
we have
\bigl\|\frac{\mathrm{d}(\nu P^{k})}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-1\bigr\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq\varepsilon

and thus
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu P^{k}\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\varepsilon
.
∎

Similarly, we can establish a
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
-guarantee for the
exponential sampling considered in §
2.2
.

Lemma 3.6
.

For given
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let
P
be the
Markov kernel of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with variance
h
and target distribution

\bar{\pi}\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
. Then,
\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{\bar{K}}}\lVert\delta_{z_{0}}P^{k}-\bar{\pi}\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}\leq\varepsilon

for
k=\mathcal{O}(h^{-1}R^{2}l^{2}\log\frac{n+h^{-1}R^{2}}{\varepsilon})
. If an
initial distribution
\nu
is
M
-warm with respect to
\bar{\pi}
,
then
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu P^{k}\mathbin{\|}\bar{\pi})\leq\varepsilon
after
k=\mathcal{O}(h^{-1}R^{2}l^{2}\log\frac{M(n+h^{-1}R^{2})}{\varepsilon})

iterations.

Proof.

As before, for
z_{0}\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}
, the composition of the forward
and backward step brings
\delta_{z_{0}}
to

(\delta_{z_{0}}P)(x,t)=\mathds{1}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}(x,t)\int\frac{\exp(-nt-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert y-z\rVert^{2})\exp(-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert y-z_{0}\rVert^{2})}{(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-nt-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert y-z\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}z}\,\mathrm{d}y\,.

Using
\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert y-z\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{3}{4h}\lVert y\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{2h}\lVert z\rVert^{2}\leq\frac{3}{4h}\lVert y\rVert^{2}+\frac{3D^{2}}{2h}

(Young’s inequality), for
D:=\text{diam}\,\bar{\mathcal{K}}\lesssim Rl
,

\displaystyle\delta_{z_{0}}P

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp(\frac{3D^{2}}{2h})\exp(-nt)}{(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\int\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert y-z\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert y-z_{0}\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{4h}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{(n+1)/2}\exp(\frac{6D^{2}}{h})\exp(-nt)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\,,

where the last line follows from

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2h}\lVert y-z\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\lVert y-z_{0}\rVert^{2}+\frac{3}{4h}\lVert y\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4h}\lVert y-2(z+z_{0})\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\lVert z_{0}\rVert^{2}+\frac{1}{h}\lVert z+z_{0}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4h}\lVert y-2(z+z_{0})\rVert^{2}+\frac{4D^{2}}{h}\,.

Dividing
\delta_{z_{0}}P
by
\bar{\pi}(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
,

\frac{\delta_{z_{0}}P}{\bar{\pi}}\leq 2^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\exp(6h^{-1}D^{2})\,,

which implies
\mathcal{R}_{q}(\delta_{z_{0}}P\mathbin{\|}\bar{\pi})\lesssim\frac{q}{q-1}\,(n+h^{-1}D^{2})
.
Noting that the truncated distribution
\bar{\pi}
has a bounded
support, so
C_{\mathsf{LSI}}(\bar{\pi})=\mathcal{O}(D^{2})
, the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
can achieve

\mathcal{R}_{q}(\delta_{z_{0}}P^{k}\mathbin{\|}\bar{\pi})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{M}

after
k=\mathcal{O}(qh^{-1}D^{2}\log\frac{M(n+h^{-1}D^{2})}{\varepsilon})
iterations,
and

\sup_{z_{0}\in\mathcal{\bar{K}}}\lVert\delta_{z_{0}}P^{k}-\bar{\pi}\rVert_{\mathsf{TV}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{M}\,.

Hence, one can complete the proof using Lemma
3.4

with
\bigl\|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\bar{\pi}}-1\bigr\|_{L_{\infty}}\leq M
.
∎

3.2.2
Complexity of implementing the backward step

We recall the backward step: for
\tau=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{h+\sigma^{2}}<1
,

y_{\tau}=\tau y
, and
h_{\tau}=\tau h
,

v\sim\mu^{V|W=w}\propto\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lVert w-v\rVert^{2}\bigr)\big|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\propto\bigl[\mathcal{N}(y_{\tau},h_{\tau}I_{n})\times\mathcal{N}(s-\rho h,h)\bigr]\big|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,.

Since we use the proposal
\mathcal{N}(y_{\tau},h_{\tau}I_{n})\times\mathcal{N}(s-\rho h,h)

for rejection sampling, the success probability of each trial at
w=(y,v)

is

\ell(w):=\frac{1}{(2\pi h_{\tau})^{n/2}(2\pi h)^{1/2}}\,\int_{\mathcal{\bar{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2h_{\tau}}\,\bigl\|x-y_{\tau}\bigr\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lvert t-(s-\rho h)\rvert^{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\,.

Density after taking the forward step.

As in Lemma
2.11
, we deduce the density of

\mu^{W}=\mu^{V}*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})
by computing the convolution
directly.

Lemma 3.7

(Density of
\mu^{W}
)
.

For
\mu^{V}\propto\exp(-\frac{\lVert x\rVert^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}-\rho t)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
,

\displaystyle\mu^{W}(y,s)

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2h_{\tau}}\,\lVert x-y_{\tau}\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lvert t-(s-\rho h)\rvert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\cdot(2\pi h)^{(n+1)/2}}\,\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y_{\tau}\rVert^{2}\bigr)\exp\bigl(-\rho s+\frac{1}{2}\,\rho^{2}h\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau^{n/2}\ell(y,s)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\,\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y_{\tau}\rVert^{2}\bigr)\exp\bigl(-\rho s+\frac{1}{2}\,\rho^{2}h\bigr)\,,

We now claim that the following is the
effective domain
of

\mu^{W}
:

\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\tau^{-1}I_{n}\\
&1\end{array}}}\right]\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta}+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
\rho h\end{array}}}\right]\,.

(3.2)

Lemma 3.8
.

In the setting of Problem
3.1
,
for
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
in (
3.2
), if
\delta\geq 24hn
,
then

\mu^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq\exp\bigl(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2h}+24\delta n+hn^{2}\bigr)\,.

Proof.

Using the density formula of
\mu^{W}
in Lemma
3.7
,
for
v=(x,t)
,
w=(y,s)
, and
\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta}^{c}:=(\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta})^{c}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\cdot\mu^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi h)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}}\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c}}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2h_{\tau}}\,\bigl\|x-y_{\tau}\bigr\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lvert t-(s-\rho h)\rvert^{2}\bigr)\exp\bigl(-\frac{\lVert y_{\tau}\rVert^{2}}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}-\rho s+\frac{\rho^{2}h}{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{=}\frac{\tau^{-n/2}}{(2\pi h_{\tau})^{n/2}(2\pi h)^{1/2}}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta}^{c}}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2h_{\tau}}\,\bigl\|x-y\bigr\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lvert t-s\rvert^{2}\bigr)\exp\bigl(-\frac{\lVert y\rVert^{2}}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}-\rho s-\frac{\rho^{2}h}{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\mathcal{\bar{K}}_{\delta}^{c}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\frac{1}{(2\pi h_{\tau})^{n/2}(2\pi h)^{1/2}}\int_{\mathcal{H}(w)}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2h_{\tau}}\,\bigl\|x-y\bigr\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2h}\,\lvert t-s\rvert^{2}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\leq\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta}^{c}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\Bigl(\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\int_{d(w,\bar{\mathcal{K}})}^{\infty}\exp\bigl(-\frac{u^{2}}{2h}\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}u\Bigr)\,\mathrm{d}w=:(\#)

where in
(i)
we used the change of variables via
y\leftarrow\tau y

and
s\leftarrow s-\rho h
, and in
(ii)

\mathcal{H}(w)
denotes the supporting
half-space at
\mathsf{proj}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}(w)
containing
\bar{\mathcal{K}}

for given
w\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta}^{c}
. By the co-area formula and
integration by parts, for the
n
-dimensional Hausdorff measure
\mathcal{H}^{n}
,

\displaystyle(\#)

\displaystyle\underset{\text{coarea}}{=}\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}u)\int_{\partial\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{u}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}w)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\underset{\text{IBP}}{=}\tau^{-n/2}\Bigl[\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}u)\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\partial\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{l}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}w)\mathrm{d}l\Bigr]_{u=\delta}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\qquad+\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{u^{2}}{2h}\bigr)\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\partial\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{l}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}w)\mathrm{d}l\mathrm{d}u\,,

and the double integral term is bounded as

\displaystyle\tau^{-n/2}\int_{0}^{u}\int_{\partial\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{l}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\,\mathcal{H}^{n}(\mathrm{d}w)\mathrm{d}l

\displaystyle=\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{u}\backslash\bar{\mathcal{K}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\,\mathrm{d}w

\displaystyle\underset{\text{Lemma }\ref{lem:annealing-expansion}}{\leq}2\exp(hn^{2}+24un)\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s}\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}s\,.

Using the tail bound on
\Phi^{c}
in (
2.6
),

\tau^{-n/2}\Phi^{c}(h^{-1/2}u)\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{u}\backslash\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s)\,\mathrm{d}w

vanishes at
u=\infty
, and thus the first term of
(\#)
can bounded
by
0
. Hence,

\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\cdot\mu^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq 2\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{u^{2}}{2h}\bigr)\exp(hn^{2}+24un)\,\mathrm{d}u\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t}\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t\,.

Dividing both sides by
\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)

and using the standard bound on
\Phi^{c}
when
\delta\geq 23hn
,

\displaystyle\mu^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\delta}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi h}}e^{-\frac{s^{2}}{2h}}e^{hn^{2}+24sn}\,\mathrm{d}s=2e^{hn^{2}}\Bigl(1-\Phi\bigl(\frac{\delta-24hn}{h^{1/2}}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigl(-\frac{\delta^{2}}{2h}+24\delta n+hn^{2}\bigr)\,,

which completes the proof.
∎

This suggests taking
\delta\asymp 1/n
and
h\asymp 1/n^{2}
as in
the exponential sampling. Precisely, under the choice of
h=\tfrac{c}{24^{2}n^{2}}

and
\delta=\tfrac{c/24+t}{n}
for parameters
c,t>0
, it follows
that
\delta\geq 24hn
and

\pi^{Y}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})\leq\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+c\bigr)\,.

We control how fast
\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{u}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)

increases in
u
.

Lemma 3.9
.

In the setting of Lemma
3.8
,
for
\tau=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{h+\sigma^{2}}<1
,
s>0
, and
\rho\in(0,n]
,

\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{s}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l}\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}l\leq 2\exp(hn^{2}+24sn)\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}e^{-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l}\,\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}l\,.

Proof.

Consider when
\sigma^{2}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2n}}
. For
F:(z,l)\mapsto(\tau^{-1/2}z,l)

between
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
, and
\hat{\mathcal{K}}:=\bar{\mathcal{K}}+\frac{V(x_{0})}{n}-\frac{V_{0}}{n}
,

\displaystyle\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\mathcal{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}_{s}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\tau^{-n/2}\int_{(1+s)\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)=(1+s)^{n}\int_{F\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{(1+s)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\,(1+s)\bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}e^{13sn}\int_{F\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{(iii)}{=}e^{13sn}\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)\times\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{n})\times\mathsf{Exp}}\bigl((X,T)\in F\hat{\mathcal{K}})\bigr)}{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{n})\times\mathsf{Exp}}\bigl((X,T)\in\hat{\mathcal{K}})\bigr)}

\displaystyle\underset{(iv)}{\leq}e^{13sn}\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)\times\bigl(\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{n})\times\mathsf{Exp}}\bigl(\lVert X\rVert\leq 1,T\geq 0\bigr)\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\underset{(v)}{\leq}\frac{e^{13sn}e^{11sn}}{1-d\sigma^{2}}\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)\leq 2e^{24sn}\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)\,.

In
(i)
, we used
\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{s}\subset(1+s)\,\hat{\mathcal{K}}
due to
B_{1}(0)\subset\hat{\mathcal{K}}
.

(ii)
follows from
l\geq-11
in
F\hat{\mathcal{K}}
. In
(iii)
,
\mathsf{Exp}

denotes the
1
-dim. exponential distribution with density proportional
to
\exp(-\rho t)|_{[-t_{0},\infty)}
for
t_{0}=\min_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}t\geq-11
.

(iv)
and
(v)
follow from
B_{1}(0)\subset\hat{\mathcal{K}}
and Chebyshev’s
inequality with
\rho\leq n
, respectively.

When
\sigma^{2}\geq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2n}}
, starting from the
(i)
-line
and using
\tau^{-n/2}\leq\exp(\nicefrac{{hn}}{{2\sigma^{2}}})
,

\displaystyle\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}_{s}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\bigl(\frac{hn}{2\sigma^{2}}+sn\bigr)\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\,(1+s)\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(hn^{2}+12sn)\int_{\hat{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert z\rVert^{2}-\rho l\bigr)\,.

We complete the proof by combining the two bounds in both cases.
∎

Per-step guarantees.

We provide the per-step query complexity and failure probability of
implementing the backward step from a warm start.

Lemma 3.10
.

In the setting of Problem
3.1
,
let
\nu
be an
M
-warm initial distribution for
\mu^{V}=\mu_{\sigma^{2},\rho}

with
\sigma^{2}>0
and
\rho\in(0,n]
. Given
k\in\mathbb{N}
and

\eta\in(0,1)
, set
Z=\frac{16kM}{\eta}(\geq 16)
,
h=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\frac{(\log\log Z)^{2}}{1200^{2}\log Z}

and
N=2Z\log^{2}Z
. Then, per iteration, the failure probability
is at most
\eta/k
, and the expected number of queries is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(M\log^{2}\tfrac{k}{\eta})
.

Proof.

Since
\nu*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})
is
M
-warm with respect to
\mu^{W}

due to
\mathrm{d}\nu/\mathrm{d}\mu^{V}\leq M
, the probability for the bad event
per iteration is
\mathbb{E}_{\nu*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})}[(1-\ell)^{N}]\leq M\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{W}}[(1-\ell)^{N}]
.
For
\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}
given in (
3.2
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{W}}[(1-\ell)^{N}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c}}\cdot+\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap[\ell\geq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\cdot+\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap[\ell\leq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\cdot

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\mu^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})+\int_{[\ell\geq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\exp(-\ell N)\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{W}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}\cap[\ell\leq N^{-1}\log(3kM/\eta)]}\frac{\tau^{n/2}\ell(y,s)\exp(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y_{\tau}\rVert^{2})\exp(-\rho s+\frac{1}{2}\,\rho^{2}h)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)}

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+c\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\frac{\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}}\tau^{n/2}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y_{\tau}\rVert^{2}-\rho s+\frac{1}{2}\,\rho^{2}h)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)}

\displaystyle\underset{(iii)}{=}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+c\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\frac{\tau^{-n/2}\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{\delta}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y\rVert^{2}-\rho s-\frac{1}{2}\,\rho^{2}h)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)}

\displaystyle\underset{(iv)}{\leq}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+c\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\,2\exp(hn^{2}+24\delta n)

\displaystyle\underset{(v)}{\leq}\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{4c}\bigr)+\frac{\eta}{3kM}+\frac{\log(3kM/\eta)}{N}\,2\exp(25t)\leq\frac{\eta}{kM}\,,

where in
(i)
we used the density formula of
\mu^{W}
in Lemma
3.7
,

(ii)
follows from Lemma
3.8
,
(iii)

is due to the change of variables, in
(iv)
we used Lemma
3.9
,
and
(v)
follows from the choices of
c=\frac{(\log\log Z)^{2}}{4\cdot 24^{2}\log Z}
,

t=\tfrac{1}{24}\log\log Z
, and
N=2Z\log^{2}Z
. Therefore,
\mathbb{E}_{\nu*\mathcal{N}(0,hI_{n+1})}[(1-\ell)^{N}]\leq\tfrac{\eta}{k}
.

The expected number of trials until the first acceptance is bounded
by
M\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{W}}[\ell^{-1}\wedge N]
, where

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n+1}}\frac{1}{\ell}\wedge N\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{W}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{1}{\ell}\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{W}+N\mu^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}^{c})

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{\int_{\widetilde{\mathcal{K}}}\tau^{n/2}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\tau\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert y_{\tau}\rVert^{2}-\rho s+\frac{1}{2}\rho^{2}h)\,\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}s}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho t)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}+N\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+c\bigr)

\displaystyle\underset{(ii)}{\leq}2\exp(hn^{2}+24\delta n)+N\exp\bigl(-\frac{t^{2}}{2c}+c\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq 4\log^{2}Z\,.

where
(i)
follows from Lemma
3.7
and
Lemma
3.8
, and in
(ii)
we repeat the
arguments around
(iii),(iv)
above. Therefore, the expected number
of queries per iteration is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(M\log^{2}\tfrac{k}{\eta})
.
∎

Combining the convergence rate and per-step guarantees of the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
,
we establish the query complexity of sampling from the annealing distribution
from an
M
-warm start:

Theorem 3.11
.

In the setting of Problem
3.1

without knowledge of
x_{0}
and
V_{0}
, for any given
\eta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

k\in\mathbb{N}
defined below,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with
h=(1200^{2}n^{2}\log\frac{16kM}{\eta})^{-1}
,

N=\frac{32kM}{\eta}\log^{2}\frac{16kM}{\eta}
, and initial distribution

\mu_{0}
which is
M
-warm with respect to
\mu:=\mu_{\sigma^{2},\rho}

(where
\sigma>0
and
\rho\in(0,n]
) achieves
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\mu_{k}\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\varepsilon

after
k=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log^{2}\frac{MR}{\eta\varepsilon})

iterations, where
\mu_{k}
is the law of
k
-th iterate. With probability
at least
1-\eta
, the algorithm iterates
k
times successfully,
using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(Mn^{2}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log^{4}\frac{R}{\eta\varepsilon})

expected number of evaluation queries in total.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.5
,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with given
h

should iterate
k=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log^{2}\frac{MR}{\eta\varepsilon})

times to achieve
\varepsilon
-distance in
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
. Under the
choice of
h
and
N
as claimed, each iteration succeeds with probability

1-\nicefrac{{\eta}}{{k}}
and uses
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(M\log^{2}\nicefrac{{k}}{{\eta}})

queries in expectation by Lemma
3.10
. Therefore,
throughout
k
iterations, the algorithm succeeds with probability

1-\eta
, using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(Mn^{2}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log^{4}\frac{R}{\eta\varepsilon})

queries.
∎

3.3
Rényi-infinity sampling for logconcave distributions

Now that we have a sampler for the annealing distribution with
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
-guarantee,
we analyze a query complexity of each phase in
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
.

Initialization.

In the initialization where
\sigma^{2}=1/n
and
\rho=1/l
, we
run
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with initial distribution
\mu_{0}=\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}

and target
\mu_{1}\propto\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-t/l)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
.
Recall that
l=\log\frac{2e}{\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 3.12

(Initialization)
.

Rejection sampling for the initial

\mu_{0}\propto\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
takes

\mathcal{O}(l)
queries in expectation. Given
\eta\in(0,1)
,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}

with

initial
\mu_{0}
, target
\mu_{1}\propto\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-t/l)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
,

h^{-1}=\widetilde{\Theta}(n^{2}\log\frac{k}{\eta})
,
N=\widetilde{\Theta}(k/\eta)
,
and
k=\widetilde{\Theta}(n^{2}l^{2}\log^{4}\frac{MR}{\eta})
iterates
k

times with probability at least
1-\eta
, returning a sample with
law
\bar{\mu}_{1}
satisfying

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\mu_{1}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{1}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{1}}-1\bigr|\leq 1\,,

where the expected number of evaluation queries used is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}l^{2}\log^{4}\frac{R}{\eta})
.

Proof.

In the initialization through rejection sampling, the expected number
of trials until the first acceptance is bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x\cdot(15+13l)}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{l\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\lVert x\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x}{\int_{B_{1}(0)}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x}=l\,\bigl(\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{N}(0,n^{-1}I_{n})}(\lVert X\rVert\leq 1)\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\leq}l\,(1-e^{-c\sqrt{n}})^{-1}=\mathcal{O}(l)\,,

where
(i)
follows from Paouris’ lemma (Lemma
A.8
)
for some universal constant
c
.

The warmness of
\mu_{0}
with respect to
\mu_{1}
is bounded as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{(x,t)\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\lVert x\rVert^{2}-t/l)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\lVert x\rVert^{2}-t/l)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(t/l)\,\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\lVert x\rVert^{2}-t/l)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{n}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2})\,\mathrm{d}x}\lesssim 1\,.

Thus,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with initial
\mu_{0}
and target
\mu_{1}
can
be run with target error of
\log 2
in the
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
-divergence,
under a suitable choice of parameters given in Theorem
3.11
.
∎

Phase I.

In the first phase (
\sigma^{2}\in[\frac{1}{n},1]
and
\rho\in[\frac{1}{l},n]
),
these parameters are updated according to

\sigma_{i+1}^{2}=\sigma_{i}^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{1}{n}\bigr)\quad\&\quad\rho_{i+1}=\rho_{i}\bigl(1+\frac{1}{ln}\bigr)\,.

Note that
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
is initialized at
\bar{\mu}_{i}
(not
\mu_{i}
)
for the target
\mu_{i+1}
.

Lemma 3.13

(Phase I)
.

Given
\eta\in(0,1)
, Phase I
of
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
started at
\bar{\mu}_{1}
succeeds with probability at
least
1-\eta
, returning a sample with law
\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{I}}

satisfying

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{I}}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathsf{I}}}-1\bigr|\leq 1\quad\text{for }\mu_{\mathsf{I}}\propto\exp(-\frac{1}{2}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,,

using
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with suitable parameters. Phase I uses
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3}l^{3}\log^{5}\frac{R}{\eta})

evaluation queries in expectation.

Proof.

Since it takes at most
ln
many inner phases to double
\sigma^{2}

and
\rho
, the total number of inner phases within Phase I is
m=\mathcal{O}(nl\log nl)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(nl)
.

The warmness between consecutive annealing distributions is bounded
as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(x,t)\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i}t)}{\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i+1}t)}\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i+1}t)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i}t)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(x,t)\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(\frac{\rho_{i}\,(t-t_{0})}{ln}\bigr)\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i}\,(t-t_{0})\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i}\,(t-t_{0})\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\underset{(i)}{\lesssim}\frac{(1+\frac{1}{n})^{n/2}\int_{T\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i}\,(t-t_{0})\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-\rho_{i}\,(t-t_{0})\bigr)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\leq\sqrt{e}\,,

where in
(i)
we used
\rho_{i}\leq n
and
t-t_{0}\lesssim l
,
and the change of variables via
T:(x,t)\mapsto((1+\frac{1}{n})^{-1/2}x,t)
.

Due to the design of the algorithm,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
always ensures that

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\mu_{i}}|\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{i}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i}}-1|\leq 1
, and
thus
\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{i}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i+1}}\leq 2\sqrt{e}
. Hence, for
each inner phase,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with target accuracy
\log 2
in
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
,
target failure probability
\eta/m
, warmness
M=2\sqrt{e}
, and
suitable parameters succeeds with probability
1-\eta/m
, returning
a sample with law
\bar{\mu}_{i+1}
such that

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{i+1}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i+1}}-1\bigr|\leq 1\,,

and using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}l^{2}\log^{4}\frac{R}{\eta})
many evaluation
queries in expectation. Repeating this argument
m
many times, we
complete the proof.
∎

Phase II.

In this second phase (
\sigma^{2}\in[1,l^{2}R^{2}]
), only
\sigma^{2}

is updated according to

\sigma_{i+1}^{2}=\sigma_{i}^{2}\bigl(1+\frac{\sigma_{i}^{2}}{l^{2}R^{2}}\bigr)\,.

Lemma 3.14

(Phase II)
.

Given
\eta\in(0,1)
, Phase II
of
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
started at
\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{I}}
succeeds with probability
at least
1-\eta
, returning a sample with law
\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}

satisfying

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathsf{II}}}-1\bigr|\leq 1\quad\text{for }\mu_{\mathsf{II}}\propto\exp(-\frac{1}{2l^{2}R^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,,

using
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with suitable parameters. Phase II uses
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}R^{2}l^{4}\log^{4}\frac{1}{\eta})

queries in expectation.

Proof.

We note that for given
\sigma^{2}
, it takes at most
l^{2}R^{2}/\sigma^{2}

inner phases to double
\sigma^{2}
, so the total number of inner
phases within Phase II is
m=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(l^{2}R^{2})
.

The warmness between consecutive annealing distributions is bounded
as

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{(x,t)\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)}{\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)}\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\leq\sqrt{e}\,,

where the last line holds, since
\lVert x\rVert^{2}\leq l^{2}R^{2}
on

\bar{\mathcal{K}}
,

\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}\lVert x\rVert^{2}\bigr)=\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\lVert x\rVert^{2}\bigr)\exp\bigl(\frac{1}{2(l^{2}R^{2}+\sigma_{i}^{2})}\lVert x\rVert^{2}\bigr)\leq\sqrt{e}\exp\bigl(-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{i}^{2}}\lVert x\rVert^{2}\bigr)\,.

As in the analysis for Phase I, this implies that due to the design
of the algorithm,

\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{i}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i+1}}\leq 2\sqrt{e}\,.

Hence, for each inner phase,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{ann}}
with target accuracy
\log 2

in
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
, target failure probability
\eta/m
, warmness

M=2\sqrt{e}
, and suitable parameters succeeds with probability

1-\eta/m
, returning a sample with law
\bar{\mu}_{i+1}
such that

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{i+1}}{\mathrm{d}\mu_{i+1}}-1\bigr|\leq 1\,,

and using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log^{4}\frac{R}{\eta})

many evaluation queries in expectation. Each doubling part requires

\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigl(\frac{l^{2}R^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\times n^{2}(\sigma^{2}\vee l^{2})\log^{4}\frac{R}{\eta}\bigr)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\bigl(n^{2}R^{2}l^{4}\log^{4}\frac{1}{\eta}\bigr)

many queries in expectation. Therefore, the claim follows from multiplying
this by
\log_{2}(lR)
.
∎

Termination.

In the last phase,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
is run with initial distribution
\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}

and target
\bar{\pi}\propto e^{-nt}|_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}
.

Lemma 3.15

(Termination)
.

Given
\eta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with
initial
distribution
\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}
,
h^{-1}=\widetilde{\Theta}(n^{2}\log\frac{k}{\eta})
,

N=\widetilde{\Theta}(k/\eta)
, and
k=\widetilde{\Theta}(n^{2}R^{2}l^{2}\log^{4}\frac{M}{\eta\varepsilon})

iterates
k
times with probability at least
1-\eta
, returning
a sample with law
\nu
satisfying

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\bar{\pi}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\bar{\pi}}-1\bigr|\leq\varepsilon\,,

where the expected number of evaluation queries is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}R^{2}l^{2}\log^{4}\frac{1}{\eta\varepsilon})
.

Proof.

As
\lVert x\rVert^{2}\leq l^{2}R^{2}
on
\bar{\mathcal{K}}
, the warmness between

\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}
and
\bar{\pi}
is bounded as

\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{\mathsf{II}}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{\pi}}\leq\sup_{(x,t)\in\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\frac{\exp(-\frac{1}{2l^{2}R^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)}{\exp(-nt)}\frac{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}{\int_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\exp(-\frac{1}{2l^{2}R^{2}}\,\lVert x\rVert^{2}-nt)\,\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}t}\leq\sqrt{e}\,,

and thus
\frac{\mathrm{d}\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}}{\mathrm{d}\bar{\pi}}\leq 2\sqrt{e}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
3.6
,
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with initial

\bar{\mu}_{\mathsf{II}}
and suitable parameters above outputs a sample
with law
\nu
such that

\operatorname{ess\,sup}_{\bar{\mathcal{K}}}\,\bigl|\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}\bar{\pi}}-1\bigr|\leq\varepsilon\,,

using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}R^{2}l^{2}\log^{4}\frac{1}{\eta\varepsilon})
evaluation
queries in expectation.
∎

Final guarantee.

As claimed in Theorem
1.5
, we prove that

\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
provides an
\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm start for
\pi(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}}
,
using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(R^{2}\vee n))
evaluation queries in expectation.

Theorem 3.16

(Warm-start generation)
.

In the setting of
Problem
3.1
, consider
\pi\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}}

given in (
exp-red
). For given
\eta,\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
succeeds with probability at least
1-\eta
, returning a
sample with law
\nu
such that
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leq\varepsilon
,
using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(R^{2}\vee n)\log^{8}\nicefrac{{1}}{{\eta\varepsilon}})

evaluation queries in expectation. In particular, if
\pi^{X}
is
well-rounded (i.e.,
R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(n)
), then we need
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3}\log^{8}\nicefrac{{1}}{{\eta\varepsilon}})

queries in expectation.

Proof.

Combining the four lemmas of this section with
\eta/4
and
\varepsilon/3

in place of
\eta
and
\varepsilon
, we conclude that by the union bound,
the failure probability is at most
\eta
. Next, the choice of
l=\mathcal{O}(\log\frac{1}{\varepsilon})

leads to

\sup_{\mathcal{K}}\frac{\bar{\pi}}{\pi}\leq\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{\pi}(Z\in\bar{\mathcal{K}})}\leq 1+\frac{2\varepsilon}{3}\,.

Therefore, the claim follow from

\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leq\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\bar{\pi})+\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\bar{\pi}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{3}+\log\bigl(1+\frac{2\varepsilon}{3}\bigr)\leq\varepsilon\,.\qed

4
Application 1: Rounding logconcave distributions

We have shown that the query complexity of generating a warm start
(in fact, sampling from
\pi^{X}
with
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}
-guarantee)
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2}(R^{2}\vee n))
. However,
R
could be arbitrarily
large so this query complexity is not actually polynomial in the problem
parameters. Hence, prior work combines sampling algorithms with a

rounding
procedure, which makes
\pi^{X}
less skewed (e.g.,

R^{2}=\operatorname{poly}n
). For instance, near-isotropic rounding makes
\operatorname{Cov}\pi^{X}

nearly the identity, under which
\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{X}}[\lVert X-\mathbb{E}X\rVert^{2}]\approx n
.

Problem 4.1

(Isotropic rouding)
.

Assume access
to a well-defined function oracle
\mathsf{Eval}_{x_{0},R}(V)
for convex

V:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\overline{\mathbb{R}}
. Given
\mathsf{s}\geq 1
, what is the query
complexity of finding an affine transformation
F:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such
that
F_{\#}\pi^{X}
satisfies

\mathsf{s}^{-1}I_{n}\preceq\operatorname{Cov}F_{\#}\pi^{X}=F[\operatorname{Cov}\pi^{X}]F^{\mathsf{T}}\preceq\mathsf{s}I_{n}\,.

We may assume that
\min V=0
, since it can be found using the evaluation
oracle when
x_{0}
is given. This problem is equivalent to estimating
the covariance of
\pi^{X}
accurately, since setting
F=[\operatorname{Cov}\pi^{X}]^{-1/2}

leads to
\operatorname{Cov}F_{\#}\pi^{X}=I_{n}
. The prior best complexity of
isotropic rounding is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{4})
by
[
LV06a
]
,
and in this section we improve this to
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3.5})
, as claimed
in Theorem
1.7
.

Theorem 4.2
.

In the setting of Problem
4.1
,
there exists a randomized algorithm with query complexity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3.5}\operatorname{polylog}R)

that finds an affine map
F^{X}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
F^{X}\pi^{X}

is
1.01
-isotropic with probability at least
1-\mathcal{O}(n^{-1/2})
.

Setup.

By translation, we assume throughout this section that
x_{0}=0

and that
\mathcal{K}=\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}:V(x)-11n\leq nt\}
contains
B_{1}^{n+1}(0)
.
We now introduce several notation for a concise description. In the
first part of the algorithm, we mainly work with
\mathrm{d}\nu^{X}\propto\exp(-V)|_{\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}}\,\mathrm{d}x

truncated to the ground set
\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}
, called the
grounded
distribution
. One can think of it as a core of
\pi^{X}
in that
(1)
\nu^{X}
is
1.01
-warm with respect to
\pi^{X}
(i.e.,
\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\nu^{X}\mathbin{\|}\pi^{X})\leq\log 1.01
),
and (2) the values of
V
over the support of
\nu^{X}
is within

10n
from
\min V=0
(i.e.,
\max_{\operatorname{supp}\nu^{X}}V\leq 10n
). The
first property immediately follows from

\sup_{\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}}\frac{\nu^{X}}{\pi^{X}}\leq\frac{\int\exp(-V)}{\int_{\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}}\exp(-V)}=\bigl(\mathbb{P}_{\pi^{X}}(X\in\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g})\bigr)^{-1}\leq 1.01\,,

(4.1)

where the last inequality is due to Lemma
A.6
(with

\beta=10
). We also denote by
\nu_{r}^{X}\propto\exp(-V)|_{\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}\cap B_{r}(0)}

the grounded distribution truncated to the ball
B_{r}(0)
. Hereafter,
we use
\mathsf{L}_{g}
to indicate
\mathsf{L}_{\pi^{X},g}
if there is
no confusion.

For each of
\pi^{X},\nu^{X},\nu_{r}^{X}
, its exponential counterpart
can be written as, for the cylinders
C_{g}:=\mathsf{L}_{g}\times\mathbb{R}

and
C_{r}:=B_{r}(0)\times\mathbb{R}
,

\pi(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}}\,,\qquad\nu(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}\cap C_{g}}\,,\qquad\nu_{r}(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{K}\cap C_{g}\cap C_{r}}\,.

We use
F^{X}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
F:\mathbb{R}^{n+1}\to\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
to denote an
affine transformation and its embedding into
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
defined by

F(x,t):=(F^{X}(x),t)
, respectively. Then, for
W:=V\circ(F^{X})^{-1}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle F_{\#}\pi(x,t)

\displaystyle\propto\exp(-nt)|_{F\mathcal{K}}\text{ where }F\mathcal{K}=\{(x,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}:W(x)\leq nt\}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\#}\nu(x,t)

\displaystyle\propto\exp(-nt)|_{F\mathcal{K}\cap FC_{g}}\text{ where }FC_{g}=F^{X}\mathsf{L}_{g}\times\mathbb{R}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\#}\nu_{r}(x,t)

\displaystyle\propto\exp(-nt)|_{F\mathcal{K}\cap FC_{g}\cap FC_{r}}\text{ where }FC_{r}=F^{X}B_{r}(0)\times\mathbb{R}\,.

Note that
(F_{\#}\pi)^{X}=F_{\#}^{X}\pi^{X}\propto\exp(-W)
,
(F_{\#}\nu)^{X}=F_{\#}^{X}\nu^{X}\propto\exp(-W)|_{F^{X}\mathsf{L}_{g}}
,
and
(F_{\#}\nu_{r})^{X}=F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{r}^{X}\propto\exp(-W)|_{F^{X}(\mathsf{L}_{g}\cap B_{r})}
.
Lastly, it clearly holds that for
\mu\in\{\pi,\nu,\nu_{r}\}
,

\operatorname{Cov}F_{\#}\mu=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\operatorname{Cov}F_{\#}^{X}\mu^{X}&*\\
*&\operatorname{Cov}\mu^{T}\end{array}}}\right]\,.

Roadmap to isotropy.

At the high level, we gradually make
\pi^{X}
less skewed through
an iterative process of sampling, estimating an approximate covariance
matrix, and applying a suitable affine transformation. Assuming that
Algorithm
4
(
\mathsf{Isotropize}
) can
solve Problem
4.1
when
R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(n)

by using
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3})
many queries, we provide a brief overview
of the entire rounding algorithm. Similar to our warm-start generation
algorithm, for
\delta:=1+n^{-1/2}>1
and
D:=2R\log 400
, this algorithm
gradually isotropizes a sequence
\{\mu_{i}\}
of distributions,

\nu_{1}^{X}\to\nu_{\delta}^{X}\to\nu_{\delta^{2}}^{X}\to\cdots\to\nu_{D}^{X}\to\nu^{X}\to\pi^{X}
,
passing along an affine map
F^{X}
such that if
F_{\#}^{X}\mu_{i}

is near-isotropic, then
H_{\#}^{X}\mu_{i+1}
(where
H^{X}
is
a slight modification of
F^{X}
) satisfies regularity conditions:

\displaystyle(\mathsf{LR})\text{ Lower regularity:}

\displaystyle\quad\text{the ground set of $H_{\#}^{X}\mu_{i+1}$ contains }B_{1}^{n}(x_{0})\ \text{for some }x_{0}\,.

\displaystyle(\mathsf{UR})\text{ Upper regularity:}

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}_{H_{\#}^{X}\mu_{i+1}}[\lVert X-x_{0}\rVert^{2}]\leq R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(n)\,.

This ensures that sampling from
H_{\#}^{X}\mu_{i+1}
can be achieved
with moderate complexity. We now provide a summary of the rounding
algorithm:

1.

Step 1
: Make
\nu_{D}^{X}
near-isotropic.

(a)

Run
\mathsf{Isotropize}
on
\nu_{1}^{X}
(which clearly satisfies

\mathsf{(LR)}
and
\mathsf{(UR)}
with
R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
) to obtain an
affine map
F^{X}
such that
F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{1}^{X}
is near-isotropic.

(b)

Repeat while
r\leq D=\mathcal{O}(R)
: if
F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{r}^{X}
is near-isotropic,
then run
\mathsf{Isotropize}
on
(4F^{X})_{\#}\nu_{\delta r}^{X}
,
and set
F^{X}\leftarrow 4F^{X}
and
r\leftarrow\delta r
.

i.

Justified by Lemma
4.12
: If
F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{r}^{X}

is near-isotropic, then
(4F^{X})_{\#}\nu_{\delta r}^{X}
satisfies

\mathsf{(LR)}
and
\mathsf{(UR)}
with
R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(n)
.

2.

Step 2
: Given a near-isotropic
F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{D}^{X}
,
make
\nu^{X}
near-isotropic.

(a)

Run
\mathsf{Isotropize}
on
(4F^{X})_{\#}\nu^{X}
to obtain a new
map
F^{X}
that makes
F_{\#}^{X}\nu^{X}
near-isotropic.

i.

Justified by Lemma
4.14
: If
F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{D}^{X}

is near-isotropic, then
(4F^{X})_{\#}\nu^{X}
satisfies
\mathsf{(LR)}

and
\mathsf{(UR})
with
R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(n)
.

3.

Step 3
: Given a near-isotropic
F_{\#}^{X}\nu^{X}
,
make
\pi^{X}
near-isotropic.

(a)

Draw
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n)
samples from
(4F^{X})_{\#}\pi^{X}
to estimate
its covariance accurately.

i.

Justified by Lemma
4.15
: If
F_{\#}^{X}\nu^{X}
is near-isotropic,
then
(4F^{X})_{\#}\pi^{X}
satisfies
\mathsf{(LR)}
and
\lVert\operatorname{Cov}F_{\#}^{X}\pi^{X}\rVert=\mathcal{O}(1)
.

Step 1 and 2 would use
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3}\times\sqrt{n}\log D)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3.5}\log D)

queries, as
\mathsf{Isotropize}
is called
\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{n}\log D)
times
in Step 1 and 2. In Step 3, as the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with an
\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm
start requires
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{2})
queries per sample, Step 3 uses
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3})

queries in total. Therefore, the final complexity of this algorithm
would be
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3.5}\operatorname{polylog}\nicefrac{{R}}{{\eta}})
for a target
failure probability
\eta
.

Remark 4.3

(Failure of samplers)
.

Recall that the mixing time of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
has
a poly-logarithmic dependence on the target failure probability
\eta>0
.
Since the number
k
of iterations (of
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
) throughout the
algorithm is polynomial in the problem parameters (e.g.,
n,R
),
we can, for instance, simply set
\eta=1/(kn^{100})
without affecting
the final query complexity by more than polylogarithmic factors.

4.1
Isotropic rounding of a well-rounded distribution

In this section, we analyze the aforementioned algorithm
\mathsf{Isotropize}
,
which finds an affine map
F^{X}
making a well-rounded distribution
near-isotropic. We state an exact setup for
\mathsf{Isotropize}
.

Assumption 4.4
.

Given access to an evaluation oracle for
convex
U:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\overline{\mathbb{R}}
with
\min U\geq 0
, and distribution

\mathrm{d}\mu^{X}\propto\exp(-U)\,\mathrm{d}x
, assume that
B_{1}^{n}(x_{0})\subset\mathsf{L}_{\mu^{X},g}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:U(x)-\min U\leq 10n\}

for known
x_{0}
and
\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{X}}[\lVert X-x_{0}\rVert^{2}]\leq R^{2}=C^{2}n

with a known constant
C\geq 1
, and that
U(x)\leq 10n
for any
x\in\operatorname{supp}\mu^{X}

(i.e.,
\operatorname{supp}\mu^{X}=\mathsf{L}_{\mu^{X},g}
).

We note that
\mu(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{S}}
for
\mathcal{S}:=\{(x,t):U(x)-11n\leq nt\}

has an important property that the slice of
\operatorname{supp}\mu
at
t=-1

recovers
\operatorname{supp}\mu^{X}
, which simply follows from
\min U\geq\min V=0
.
Also, this setup can represent
F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{r}^{X}
and
F_{\#}^{X}\nu^{X}
.
For instance,
\mu^{X}=F_{\#}^{X}\nu_{r}^{X}
can be obtained by
setting
U=V\circ(F^{X})^{-1}/\mathds{1}[x\in F^{X}(\mathsf{L}_{g}\cap B_{r}(0))]
.

Rounding algorithm,
\mathsf{Isotropize}
.

This algorithm generalizes the approach from
[
JLLV21
]
,
a rounding algorithm for well-rounded uniform distributions over a
convex body, following a modified version of their algorithm studied
in
[
KZ24
]
for streamlined analysis.

Algorithm
4
begins with
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
to generate
an
\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm start for
\mu
, using
n^{3}
queries (Theorem
3.16
).
The while-loops then proceeds as follows: for
r:=\operatorname{inrad}\mu^{X}
,
the largest radius of a ball contained in
\operatorname{supp}\mu^{X}=\mathsf{L}_{\mu^{X},g}
,
we
can
run the sampler
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
(with the
\mathcal{O}(1)
-warm
start and target failure probability
n^{-\mathcal{O}(1)}
) to generate
r^{2}

samples distributed according to
\mu
, since under Assumption
4.4
,

\mu^{X}
meets all conditions required by Problem
2.1
.
We then estimate an approximate covariance matrix
\overline{\Sigma}

of
\mu^{X}
(using these
r^{2}
samples) such that
\lvert\overline{\Sigma}-\Sigma\rvert\precsim nI_{n}
,
where
\Sigma=\operatorname{Cov}\mu^{X}
. This procedure requires
r^{2}\times n^{2}\lVert\Sigma\rVert/r^{2}=n^{2}\lVert\Sigma\rVert

many queries in expectation. Next, we compute the eigenvalue/vectors
of
\overline{\Sigma}
and double the subspace spanned by the eigenvectors
with eigenvalues less than
n
. One iteration of this inner loop
achieves two key properties: (
\mathsf{P1}
) the largest eigenvalue
of the covariance
\Sigma
increases by at most
n
, and (
\mathsf{P2}
)

r=\operatorname{inrad}\mu^{X}

almost
doubles.

This algorithm repeats this inner loop (i.e., sampling–estimation–scaling)
until
r^{2}
reaches
n
. As
r
almost doubles every inner loop,
the inner loop iterates at most
\mathcal{O}(\log n)
times. Since the operator
norm of the initial covariance matrix is
\mathcal{O}(n)
due to Assumption
4.4
,
the operator norm will remain less than
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n)
throughout the
algorithm, and thus the query complexity of the inner loop is bounded
by
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(n^{3})
.

Input:
distribution
\mu\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\{(x,t):U(x)-11n\leq nt\}}

over
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
such that
B_{1}(0)\subset\operatorname{supp}\mu^{X}=\mathsf{L}_{\mu^{X},g}

and
\mathbb{E}_{\mu^{X}}[\lVert X\rVert^{2}]\leq R^{2}=\mathcal{O}(n)
.

Output:
near-isotropic distribution

F_{\#}\mu_{\text{last}}
.

1:

Run
\mathsf{Tilted\ Gaussian\ Cooling}
to obtain
Z_{0}=(X_{0},T_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
with

\mathcal{R}_{\infty}(\operatorname{law}Z_{0}\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\log 2
.

2:

Let
r_{1}=1
,
\mu_{1}:=\mu
, and
i=1
.

3:

while

r_{i}^{2}\lesssim n

do

4:

For
k_{i}\asymp r_{i}^{2}\operatorname{polylog}n
, draw
\{Z_{j}=(X_{j},T_{j})\}_{j\in[k_{i}]}\leftarrow\mathsf{PS}_{N_{i}}\bigl(\mu_{i},\delta_{Z_{0}},h_{i}\bigr)

with
N_{i}\asymp r_{i}^{-2}n^{3}\log n
and
h_{i}\asymp r_{i}^{2}(n^{2}\log(r_{i}n))^{-1}
.

5:

Estimate
\overline{\mathsf{m}}_{i}=\frac{1}{k_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{i}}X_{j}

and
\overline{\Sigma}_{i}=\frac{1}{k_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{i}}(X_{j}-\overline{\mathsf{m}}_{i})^{\otimes 2}
.

6:

Compute
M_{i}=I_{n}+P_{i}
, where
P_{i}
is the orthogonal
projection to the subspace spanned by eigenvectors of
\overline{\Sigma}_{i}

with eigenvalue at most
n
. Define
M_{i}^{*}(x,t):=(M_{i}x,t)
.

7:

Set
\mu_{i+1}=(M_{i}^{*})_{\#}\mu_{i}
,
X_{0}\leftarrow M_{i}X_{0}
,

r_{i+1}=2r_{i}(1-\nicefrac{{1}}{{\log n}})
, and
i\leftarrow i+1
.

8:

end

while

9:

Draw
\Theta(n\operatorname{polylog}n)
samples via
\mathsf{PS}_{N}(\mu_{\text{last}},\delta_{Z_{0}},h)

with
N\asymp n^{2}\operatorname{polylog}n
and
h\asymp(n\log n)^{-1}
, and
use their
X
-part to estimate the mean
\overline{\mathsf{m}}
and
covariance
\overline{\Sigma}
. For
F(x,t):=(\overline{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(x-\overline{\mathsf{m}}),t)
,
return
F_{\#}\mu_{\text{last}}
.

Algorithm 4

\mathsf{Isotropize}

Covariance estimation using dependent samples.

We now specify how we can accurately estimate the target covariance

\Sigma
using
r^{2}
many
dependent
samples drawn by the

\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
. Letting
P
be the Markov kernel of the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
with
target
\mu(x,t)\propto\exp(-nt)|_{\mathcal{S}}
and step size
h\asymp r^{2}/n^{2}
,
we showed in Lemma
2.9
that
\mathsf{PS}
has contraction
in
\chi^{2}
-divergence, i.e., for any distribution
\nu\ll\mu
,

\chi^{2}(\nu P\mathbin{\|}\mu)\leq\frac{\chi^{2}(\nu\mathbin{\|}\mu)}{\bigl(1+h/C_{\mathsf{PI}}(\mu)\bigr)^{2}}\,,

which implies that the spectral gap of this Markov chain is at least

hC_{\mathsf{PI}}^{-1}=\Omega(r^{2}n^{-2}C_{\mathsf{PI}}^{-1})
. Hence, a new Markov chain
with kernel
P^{N}
for
N\asymp r^{-2}n^{2}C_{\mathsf{PI}}
(i.e., composition
of the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}

N
-times) has a spectral gap at least
0.99
(arbitrarily
close to
1
). Hereafter,
\mathsf{PS}_{N}(\mu,\nu,h)
denotes a Markov
chain with kernel
P^{N}
, initial distribution
\nu
, and target
distribution
\mu
.

When the
\mathsf{PS}_{\textup{exp}}
samples from
\mu
in Line
4
,
it takes a sample every
N
iteration; in other words, it draws
\Theta(r_{i}^{2}\operatorname{polylog}n)

consecutive samples from the Markov chain with kernel
P^{N}
. Then,
the following result allows us to estimate a covariance matrix of

\mu
with a provable guarantee in Line
5

when using dependent samples under a spectral-gap condition:

Lemma 4.5

(
[
KZ24
, Theorem 8]
)
.

Let
P
be a reversible Markov chain on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with stationary distribution

\mu
and spectral gap
\lambda
. Let
(Z_{i})_{i\in[k]}
be a sequence
of outputs given by
P
with initial distribution
\mu
. If
\mu

is logconcave and
\lambda\geq 0.99
, then the covariance estimator
\overline{\Sigma}=k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(Z_{i}-\overline{\mathsf{m}})^{\otimes 2}

for
\overline{\mathsf{m}}=k^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}Z_{i}
satisfies that
for any
\varepsilon>0
and
\delta\in(0,n]
, with probability at least

1-\mathcal{O}(n^{-1})
,
